NAME: Balcomb Greene
BIO: Balcomb Greene was an American artist and teacher. He and his wife, artist Gertrude Glass Greene, were heavily involved in political activism to promote mainstream acceptance of abstract art and were founding members of the American Abstract Artists organization. His early style was completely non-objective.

NAME: Leo "Slim" Krikorian
BIO: Leo Krikorian first heard about Black Mountain College from a girl with whom he was having a cup of coffee in Los Angeles: “She talked about Black Mountain College for maybe five, ten minutes, and I really got interested.” At the time he was studying photography at the Art Center in Los Angeles. He wrote for an application and returned it with some of his photographs. He recalled that he did not feel he had the requirements to enter conventional colleges.

NAME: Hyalie Yamins
BIO: Hyalie was a student from 1936 to 1941. In 1937, Hyalie participated in Winterset: a play in three acts by Maxwell Anderson as "First Girl." After college she taught speech therapy at Martin Hall in Bristol, Rhode Island where she met her husband, Leo Greene. Greene was drafted and Hyalie often sent updates to the college bulletin to share their whereabouts. She taught speech therapy all her life and was also a long time member of Sakonet Preservation Association, serving as the Association Treasurer.
Relations: Sister: Sybil Eleanor Yamins
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: English/ Writing
Role: Student
Start Year: 1936.0
End Year: 1941.0

NAME: Gwendolyn "Gwen" Currier
BIO: Gwendolyn Currier Jamieson was born on November 16, 1921 in Moulmein, Burma to Raymond Currier and Edith Gould. Her father was secretary to The American Mission to Lepers, Inc. and was located in Burma until Gwendolyn was two. She attended Westfield High School from 1936-1939, and then New Jersey College for Women from 1939-1941.
Relations: Husband: Edward Jamieson, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Gwendolyn in 1997 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

Spring semester, 1942-43: Introductory Chemistry (Hansgirg), Operas of Mozart (Jalowetz), Ensemble (Jalowetz), Tutorial in German (Straus), Cello (Lowinsky), Community Work (Wood). Summer semester, 1943: Ensemble (Cohen), Composition (Cohen), German Literature (Straus), Chorus (Jalowetz), Psychology and Psychopathy of Language (Straus). Fall semester, 1943-44: Ensemble (Cohen, Jalowetz), Harmony (Hindemith) (Cohen), Classical Symphony (Jalowetz), Music History (Lowinsky), Acoustics (Hansgirg), Chorus (Jalowetz), German reading (Straus). Winter semester, 1944: Chamber Music (Cohen, Lowinsky, Jalowetz), Counterpoint (Jalowetz), Bach Seminar (Lowinsky), A Capella (Lowinsky), History of Music (Lowinsky), Cello (Lowinsky). NAME: William "Bill" Berry
BIO: William took general coursework to prepare for his service in the armed forces, such as math, English, and Spanish. He also took coursework in dramatics throughout his three semesters at the college. He participated in at least three plays, where he is listed on the playbill. He joined the US Air Corps upon leaving the college and continued to write to the bulletin to share his experience through bootcamp and where he was stationed.

NAME: Elizabeth "Slats" Slater
BIO: Elizabeth Jane Slater was a student at the college from 1940 to 1945. She was lovingly known by students and faculty as Jane, or “Slater,” “Slats,” “Slatso,” and other variations on her last name. She was very active in the arts and drama departments and spent time between the two, creating textiles and costumes for stage production. She worked in many mediums, including drawing, painting, graphic arts, weaving, and quilting.

NAME: David Sear
BIO: David Sear (b. 1932) is a folk singer and radio host. He enrolled at Black Mountain College to record African American folk music in the area. He was befriended by the Reverend Laurence Daugherty and his Gospel Stars and traveled with them on tours. In New York, he formed a partnership Dillingham & Sear to tune and repair pianos, clavichords and harpsichords. His group, the American Folk Trio, brought folk music to children through the YoungAudiences programs mostly in the New York area, but also throughout the United States. He had a program, Adventures in Folk Music, on WNYC in which he combined folk and Baroque music. He later changed the title to Folk Music Almanac. He also ran a folk music concert series at the Spencertown Academy in Spencertown, New York.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Music
Role: Student
Start Year: 1950.0
End Year: 1951.0

NAME: Edgar Kaufmann, Jr.
BIO: Edgar Kaufmann, Jr. had a privileged childhood in Pittsburgh where his father owned Kaufmann's department store. He studied painting in New York, London, Vienna and Florence. On his return to the United States he was an apprentice at Frank Lloyd Wright's Taliesin from 1933-34. It was on a visit to his son that Kaufmann's father commissioned Wright to design his Bear Run home Fallingwater. Kaufmann worked for a time in his father's store in Pittsburgh.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Architecture
Role: Guest Faculty
Start Year: 1948.0
End Year: 1948.0

NAME: Mary Nee

NAME: Mary "MC" Richards
BIO: Mary Caroline “M.C.” Richards was hired by Black Mountain College in the fall of 1945 to teach literature and writing. She remained at the college through the summer of 1952 except for the summer of 1946, a sabbatical from the fall of 1948 through the 1949 summer session, and the 1952 spring semester. Richards’ husband Albert William Levi, whom she married in 1945, taught philosophy and social sciences.
Relations: Husband: Albert Levi, Mary Emma Harris interviewed MC in 1998 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

Focus: English/ Writing
Role: Faculty

End Year: 1952.0

NAME: Herbert Miller
BIO: Herbert A. Miller was a very active faculty member at the college and gave many informal talks on philosophy and sociology. He also brought students to conferences on Race Relations and helped in College programming to raise money for various, local Negro community members.

NAME: Lyonel Feininger
BIO: Lyonel Charles Feininger was a German-American painter, and a leading exponent of Expressionism. He also worked as a caricaturist and comic strip artist. He was born and grew up in New York City, traveling to Germany at 16 to study and perfect his art. He was guest faculty in painting in the summer of 1945.

NAME: Victor D'Amico
BIO: Victor D'Amico was an American teaching artist and the founding Director of the Department of Education of the Museum of Modern Art, New York. D’Amico explored the essence of the art experience as spiritual involvement, and the ability to communicate one's most profound ideas and emotions through aesthetic expression.

NAME: David Weinrib
BIO: In 1952, with his wife Karen Karnes, Weinrib was a resident potter during the first Crafts Institute at Black Mountain College. Lasting October 15–29, 1952, the workshop also included instruction by Bernard Leach, Shoji Hamada, Dr. Soetsu Yanagi and Marguerite Wildenhain. At this time, Weinrib began experimenting in his practice of art production, creating tiles, "slab" pottery, and sculpted pots.

NAME: Josef Albers
BIO: Josef Albers was born in Bottrop, Germany. In 1905 he enrolled in a seminary at Beuren, and began teaching elementary school in 1908. NAME: Willem "Bill" de Kooning
BIO: One of the most prominent and celebrated of the Abstract Expressionist painters, Willem de Kooning's pictures typify the vigorous, gestural style of the movement. Perhaps more than any of his contemporaries, he developed a radically abstract style of painting that fused Cubism, Surrealism and Expressionism. While many of his colleagues moved from figuration to abstraction, de Kooning always painted figures, most notably women, and abstractions concurrently, making no distinction between the art historical categories. De Kooning's real subject, he insisted, was space and the figure-ground relation. De Kooning fused abstraction, figuration, and landscapes in various ways throughout the many long decades of his career, and his unceasing journey to find new forms and subjects made his overall output more eclectic than most of his colleagues. His engagement with popular culture was also unique and informed a host of post-war artists from the Neo-Dadaism of Robert Rauschenberg to the Pop Art of James Rosenquist, and younger painters such as Cecily Brown have explored the gestural eroticism of his later paintings.
Relations: Wife: Elaine de Kooning
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Guest Faculty
Start Year: 1948.0
End Year: 1948.0

NAME: Samuel "Sam" Brown, Jr.
BIO: Samuel Brown was a student from the fall of 1941 to the fall of 1945. He took most of his coursework in the physical sciences and music, ultimately focusing on music for graduation. His student file shows that he applied for many positions to teach music at other universities upon graduation. Sam attended Harvard for a year before applying to BMC. He was active on campus as a student officer and was a member of the Board of Fellows.

NAME: Adele Albert
BIO: Adele Albert attended the college for the summer of 1944.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Summer Session 1944: Cultural History (Bentley), Community Chorus (Jalowetz), English Literature (Kurtz), General Introduction to Sociology (Miller), Plato (Straus),Intro to Philosophy of Science (Straus), Drawing (Charlot)  A fantastic obituary was shared in her memory from Legacy.com, an excerpt can be seen here: Upon retiring from Fulton County (GA), she became an ardent activist. In 1984-85 she walked across the United States, as well as from Western Europe to Eastern Europe on the Walk for the People to promote peace and nuclear disarmament. Afterwards, she moved to the north Georgia mountains to continue her activism surrounded by nature. With her friend Joanne Steele, she co-founded ACE: Action for a Clean Environment, which waged successful campaigns against landfills and a medical incinerator, as well as raising awareness on many other environmental issues. An active member and for several years co-convener of the NE Georgia Peace Council, as well as WAND (Women's Action for Nuclear Disarmament), Nuclear Watch South, and the Workers Circle, Adele worked tirelessly for peace and justice. She was a frequent letter writer to local newspapers and participated in many demonstrations, some annually such as the Kings Bay Trident submarine base and the School for the Americas. She was an insatiable reader, devout vegetarian, and possessed a quick wit and superb intellect. An avid folk dancer, she credited dancing with preparing her well for her year and a half long Walk for the People. Having learned piano as a child, she maintained a love and practice of music, singing with the Atlanta Choral Guild under Robert Shaw and the Collegium Musicum community chorus at Emory University. In her 80s, she began painting in watercolors, writing poetry and her memoirs, and making spectacular mosaics.  Alternative Name: Adele Kushner
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Student
Start Year: 1944.0

NAME: Henry "Hal" Adams, Jr.
BIO: Henry "Hal" Bethune Adams, Jr. was active at the College, giving weekly news commentaries to the study body and participating in many drama events. Henry’s senior examination records and some of his class writings are in his student file and show his love for writing. He joined the student body in the fall of 1942 and was drafted in 1943. While in the service, he regularly sent updates to the Community bulletins of his time away. Though there isn't much information accessible to share facts of his life while a student or shortly after, one can see that he had a love for storytelling when looking through the College bulletins. He even mentions in one that he was writing articles, essays, and fiction, and hoping to have someone look over his work. Henry returned to complete his senior evaluations at BMC until 1947. He requested transfer records from the college to be sent to the University of Chicago, University of Michigan, Duke University, and Harvard University in 1949. He wrote to the college in 1951 from Durham, NC so it is assumed that he attended Duke University for his MA. He asked for another round of transcripts to be sent in 1953 to Washington University, University of Kentucky, Purdue University, and the University of Buffalo where he wished to continue in the study of Psychology.
Relations: Cousin: John J Gifford
COURSE TAKEN: Fall Term 1942-1943: Survey of the History of Music (Lowinsky), Advanced Spanish (deGraaff),Introductory Writing (Wunsch), Ensemble (Cohen), Piano Lessons (Lowinsky), Chorus (Jalowetz), Madrigal Group (Lowinsky), History Lectures (Schomer), Winter Quarter 1942-1943: Survey of the History of Music (Lowinsky), ElementaryHarmony and Ear Training (Cohen), A Capella Choir (Lowinsky), Chorus (Jalowetz),Advanced Spanish (de Graaff), Piano Lessons (Lowinsky), Introductory Writing(Wunsch), The Operas of Mozart (Jalowetz), Europe 1600-1800 (Bently) Spring Quarter 1942-43: The Nineteenth Century (Bentley), Music of the Renaissance and the Baroque (Lowinsky), Advanced Spanish (de Graaff), Elementary Harmony II(Cohen), Chorus (Jalowetz), A Cappella Choir (Lowinsky, Operas of Mozart (Jalowetz),Piano Lessons (Lowinsky), Community Work (Wood) Spring 1943: Psychology and Psychopathology of Lan. (Dr. Irwin Straus), Piano(Cohen), American Life and Letters (Kurtz), European Geography (Freud), DramaticProduction (Wunsch), Piano Lessons (Dr. Jalowetz), Chorus (Dr. Jalowetz), Elementary of Musical Form and Structure (Dr. Jalowetz). Fall Term 1946-47: Historical Introduction to Present Day Economic Analysis (Niebyl),Introduction to Music (Schlesinger), Mechanics & Mathematical Analysis (Dreier)Psychology of Social Issues (Wallen), World Today (Miller), Piano Lessons (No TeacherPresent on Card) Spring Term 1946-47: Historical Introduction to Present-Day Analysis (Neibyl), HumanRelations (Wallen), Writing (Richards), Reading (Richards) Printing (NoTeacher Presenton Card) Summer Term 1947: Writing (Sproul), Introduction to Sociology (Miller), Chaucer, Spenser Milton (Bell), Chorus (Sproul).

NAME: Robert Turner
BIO: Robert Chapman Turner was an American potter known for his functional pottery, sculptural vessels and inspired teaching. Born in Port Washington, New York, Turner attended Swarthmore College and graduated with a Bachelor of Arts degree in 1936. He then studied painting at the Pennsylvania Academy of Fine Arts and earned a Master of Fine Arts degree from the New York State College of Ceramics at Alfred University in 1949.

NAME: Sue Thomas Turner
BIO: Sue Turner (1913-2009) came to Black Mountain College with her husband Robert Turner, who had been hired to create a ceramics program at the college. At BMC, she took a course in theater with Wes Huss, but her primary focus was on caring for her two small sons. Sue Turner was an active supporter of Quaker values in public education. The Sue Thomas Turner Quaker Education Fund was established in her honor.

NAME: George Zabriske
BIO: George Zabriskie taught writing at the college from the fall of 1944 to the summer of 1945. At the time of teaching at the colleg he held an A.B. from Duke University and authored "The Mind's Geography" while a Guggenheim fellow of poetry in 1942-43. Zabriskie went on to author many other books and collections of poems. There are many college bulletins that share his involvement in college activities, including weekly poetry readings for the students and faculty, and serving as fire chief on the Fire Committee. While chief, he did handle one large fire in 1944.
Relations:

NAME: Victor Sprague
BIO: Victor Sprague, a marine biologist and authority marine invertebrates and their parasites, taught biology at Black Mountain from the fall of 1951 through the fall of 1952. Spague obtained his Ph.D. from the University of Illinois and taught at several schools including Louisiana State University and Young Harris College. He served in the army during World War II. Before arriving at Black Mountain, he was head of the Parasitology Division, Project 9 at the Texas A&M Research Foundation.

NAME: Harold Anderson
BIO: Harold Henry Anderson was a student at Black Mountain College for the fall semester of 1945. A recommendation letter from Mr. Erbie Bunten, of the Muskegon Public School system, shared that Harold was socialistic and an avid reader. He also shared that his family was religious and very active in the church, but that Harold held more scientific views. He had an interest in architecture and design. He was admitted as a student in the fall of 1945 and asked for a withdraw by mid-October.

NAME: Mary "Mickey" Miller
BIO: Mickey Miller (1927-2001) received her B.A. from the University of California-Los Angeles. While at Black Mountain College in the fall of 1947, she collected folk songs in the area. Those songs are included in a recording titled Mickey Miller Sings American Folk Songs (Folkways Records FA 2393) (accompanied by Beth Lomax Hawes). She obtained her master’s degree in psychology and was instrumental in forming of the Topanga Center for Human Development.

NAME: Barbara "Bobbie" Dreier
BIO: Like her husband, Barbara Loines Dreier was from a family of nonconformists, "un-regular" Christians," and adventurous thinkers. Her great-grandmother had fone to China on a clipper ship with an aunt and uncle and had stayed for five years. Her grandmother was a suffragist and a Unitarian, which Bobbie Dreier recalled "was as good as being a Communist in her day," and she married a Quaker, who was "read out of meeting for marrying such a radical girl." Two of her aunts became Christian Scientists, and her mother left the Episcopal Church after becoming interested in theosophy.
Relations: Husband: Theodore Dreier, Child: Theodore Dreier, Jr., Child: Edward Dreier, Child: Mark Dreier, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Ted and Barbara in 1974 (transcript) and 2001 (transcript, transcript) which are available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: 
Role: Family, Admin
Start Year: 1933.0

NAME: Cora Ward
BIO: Cora Kelley was a student for the summers of 1949 and 150, and also worked as a nurse at BMC. She also attended the Newcomb Art School at Tulane University, Hunter College where she obtained an MA, and the Institute of Design in Chicago in 1953. In 1955, she moved to New York and became an active part of the second generation of Abstract Expressionists. After living in New York, she became active in Louisiana and much of her work was donated to the Paul and Lulu Hilliard University Art Museum of Louisiana.

NAME: Stanley "Stan" Cooke
BIO: Stanley Cooke was a student at the college from fall 1948 to spring 1949. He was a veteran and attended on the GI bill. He noted in his application that he was interested in learning how to compose his own music. Mary Emma Harris wrote this bio for the BMC Project, "Later in New York he took classes at the Contemporary School of Music with Stefan Wolpe and Ralph Shapey and studied briefly at the Juilliard School. After leaving New York City, he worked in a factory as a toolmaker. He later became a computer programmer. He was a data processing manager and he lived in upstate New York."
Relations: Wife: Gerda Slavson, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Stanley in 2001 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

NAME: Horace "Mac" Wood
BIO: Horace McGuire Wood came to Black Mountain College (Black Mountain, NC) in 1942 to become the school's business manager and director of the work experience program. He later taught design and construction courses. After they left the college in December 1945, Mr. and Mrs. Wood continued to live at their home in the town of Black Mountain, where he conducted a house planning and building seminar for many years. (Biographical note taken from the finding aid for the Horace McGuire Wood Papers, 1933 - 1972, North Carolina State Archives.)

He graduated on November 24, 1941.

NAME: Beaumont Newhall
BIO: Beaumont Newhall was an American curator, art historian, writer, photographer. He served as the first curator of the department of photography at the Museum of Modern Art and the second director of the George Eastman Museum. His book The History of Photography remains one of the most significant accounts in the field and has become a classic photographic history textbook.

NAME: Laurence "Larry" Birns
BIO: A former defense researcher, strategist and member of the Institute for Strategic Studies in London and the All Souls College, Oxford’s military seminar, he was a senior grade public affairs officer of the U.N. Economic Commission for Latin America in Santiago, Chile. Birns also taught and lectured for 15 years in the fields of Latin American studies, comparative government, and international law at a number of U.S. and British colleges and universities.

Focus: Social Sciences

Start Year: 1950.0
End Year: 1950.0

NAME: Heinz Oberlander
BIO: Peter Oberlander (1922-2008) was a Canadian architect and Canada’s first professor of urban and regional planning. He obtained his Bachelor of Architecture degree from McGill University and his Master of Urban Planning and Ph.D. in Urban and Regional Planning from Harvard University. At Harvard, Walter Gropius suggested that Oberlander and fellow student Harry Seidler enroll for the 1946 Summer Art Institute at Black Mountain College to study with Josef Albers. At the University of British Columbia, Oberlander established Canada’s first department of Urban and Regional Planning. In Ottawa, he initiated the federal government’s Ministry of Urban Affairs. With his support and encouragement, the United Nations World Urban Forum III was held in Vancouver in 2006. Posthumously, he was awarded the United Nations Scroll of Honour Award on World Habitat Day, October 4, 2009.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Architecture
Role: Student
Start Year: 1946.0
End Year: 1946.0

NAME: Stephen Forbes
BIO: Stephen was a student from fall 1935 to spring 1942 and focused on physics. Before BMC, he attended Milton Academy 1923-1930 and Harvard College 1930-1934, and Harvard summer school 1932 and 1933. He shared in his application, "In 1932-1934 Harvard took up so much of my time I had very little left over for other things. In 1934-1935 I was sick the whole winter so I could do nothing that year. However, I used to have a great interest in numbers (progressions, factors, etc.). Also I was very much interested in the populations of cities, particularly those in the United States.”

NAME: Ernst Bacon
BIO: Ernst Bacon was an acclaimed American composer, pianist, and conductor. Born in Chicago, Bacon attended Northwestern University, the University of Chicago, and the University of California, Berkeley. Although mostly self-taught as a composer, he studied briefly with Karl Weigl in Vienna, Austria. He was on the faculty of the Eastman School of Music and the San Francisco Conservatory of Music and was appointed to head the WPA Music Project in San Francisco. He also founded the Carmel Bach Festival. His chief teaching positions were at Converse College in Spartanburg, SC (1938-45) and Syracuse University (1945-64), where he retired as Professor Emeritus.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Music
Role: Guest Faculty
Start Year: 1944.0
End Year: 1944.0

NAME: Heinrich Jalowetz
BIO: Heinrich Jalowetz was an Austrian musicologist and conductor. He was one of the core members of what became known as the Second Viennese School in the orbit of Arnold Schoenberg. He was music faculty at BMC from fall 1939 to about January of 1946 shortly before his passing.
Relations: Wife: Johanna Jalowetz, Child: Elizabeth Jalowetz, Child: Trude Guermonprez

NAME: Sandra Kocher
BIO: Sandra Kocher is the daughter of Margaret and A. Lawrence Kocher, who taught architecture at Black Mountain College from the fall of 1940 through the 1943 summer session. She was almost seven when she arrived at Black Mountain. While at Black Mountain College, Kocher designed the Studies Building, a barn, music practice cubicles, and two small houses, one for the music teacher and his wife, and one for the kitchen staff and other black workers.

NAME: Mary Washington
BIO: Mary Parks Washington heard about Black Mountain at the end of her senior year at Spelman College in Atlanta where she majored in art. Her teacher Hale Woodruff told her about a scholarship being offered by the Rosenwald Fund for study at the 1946 Summer Art Institute at Black Mountain. The college had enrolled its first black students in the summer of 1944, and Washington remembered that althoughthe South was still segregated, she felt comfortable attending.

NAME: Albert "Bill" Levi
BIO: Bill Levi was faculty of social science and philosophy from fall 1945 to fall 1950. He received an AB in sociology from Dartmouth College in 1932 and an AM and PhD from the University of Chicago. After leaving BMC, he taught at Washington University in St. Louis until his retirement. He is author of a number of books including Philosophy and the Modern World, Philosophy as Social Expression, Literature, Philosophy and the Imagination, and many others.
Relations: Wife: M.C. Richards, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Albert in 1977 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

NAME: Sydney "Syd" Carter
BIO: Sydney Carter was a student from 1933 to 1935. He had a focus in drama, creative writing, and english literature, and completed his senior examinations, but the marks weren't satisfactory. He had examiners from Harvard and George Washington University that felt he didn't have a full grasp on the history of english literature. Many notes share that he wouldn't have made a good writer and wasn't a promising student.

NAME: Peter Grippe
BIO: Peter Grippe was an American sculptor, printmaker, and painter. As a sculptor, he worked in bronze, terracotta, wire, plaster, and found objects. His "Monument to Hiroshima" series used found objects cast in bronze sculptures to evoke the chaotic humanity of the Japanese city after its incineration by atomic bomb.

NAME: Fernando "Paco" Leon
BIO: Fernando Leon (1919-2009) was an engineer. Before immigrating to the United States, he and his brother Francisco “Paco” Leon fought with the resistance in Spain in the Spanish Civil War. Francisco, who died from tuberculosis which he contracted in the war, also enrolled at Black Mountain College (BMC). Leon received his B.S. in engineering from Columbia University and his Master of Automotive Engineering from the Chrysler Institute. He worked at Chrysler on development of the XIV-2220 aircraft engine and was a founder of FOREL Equipment Co. in New York City, an exporter of steelmaking and metalworking equipment which helped set up industrial plants in Latin America and Europe under the Marshall Plan. Later he founded Om-El Corporation in Bay City, Michigan, with similar activities as FOREL. Leon was a trustee for Marlboro College in Vermont where Roland Boyden, a former BMC teacher, served in many roles. Leon was married to BMC student Eleanor “Elie” Smith Leon.
Relations: Brother: Robert Leon, Wife: Eleanore Smith, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Fernando in 2005 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Languages/ Linguistics
Role: Student
Start Year: 1939.0
End Year: 1942.0

NAME: Leonard "Knute" Stiles
BIO: Knute Stiles (1923-2009) was a painter, collagist, art critic, and writer. During World War II, he served in Alaska and thereafter spent summers working on salmon boats and organizing workers. He enrolled at Black Mountain College in 1946 and studied with Ilya Bolotowsky. In San Francisco, he was briefly co-owner with Leo Krikorian of The Place, an influential bar in San Francisco’s North Beach district that was a gathering place for Beat writers and artists. Stiles taught drawing at the San Francisco Art Institute and wrote criticism for Art Forum and Art in America. He was a founder and member of San Francisco’s East-West House, a communal living space, where many artists and writers lived.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Student
Start Year: 1946.0
End Year: 1947.0

NAME: Gertrud "Trude" Guermonprez
BIO: Trude Jalowetz Guermonprez was born in Danzig, Germany and attended the School of Fine and Applied Arts in Halle-Saale, where she studied weaving under the Bauhaus-trained artist Benita Otte. In 1933, she earned a professional diploma from the Textile Engineering School in Berlin and received a fellowship to study Scandinavian weaving in Sweden and Finland. Upon completion of her studies, Guermonprez moved to Holland where she continued to foster her understanding of technical weave structures and patterns, working for the Dutch hand weaving production studio Het Paapje.
Relations: Parent: Heinrich Jalowetz, Parent: Johanna Jalowetz, Sister: Elizabeth Jalowetz
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Faculty

End Year: 1949.0

NAME: Harold Raymond
BIO: Unable to attend the last two years of high school because of illness, Harold Raymond was tutored privately at his home in Melrose, Massachusetts. Having experienced an unconventional high school education, he was concerned that he might not be comfortable in a conventional college setting. A cousin who was teaching in New York City recommended Black Mountain College to him.
Relations: Mary Emma Harris interviewed Harold in 1997 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript, Harold Bradford Raymond Collection: Harold Bradford Raymond Collection

NAME: Marian Nacke
BIO: In Denver, Colorado, Marian Nacke Teeter read the Reader’s Digest condensed version of Louis Adamic’s article about Black Mountain College. She recalled that her family had a "radical viewpoint politically" – they were socialists – and she was not interested in traditional schools.

NAME: Warren "Pete" Jennerjahn
BIO: Warren P. “Pete” Jennerjahn enrolled at Black Mountain College in the summer of 1948 with his wife Betty, a former student. He had completed his M.S. degree in art at the University of Wisconsin, Madison. At Black Mountain in the fall he was both student and assistant to Josef Albers. In the spring and summer of 1949 he was instructor in art.

NAME: Patricia Passlof
BIO: Patricia Passlof was a student for the 1948 Summer Art Institute. After BMC, she got her BA from Cranbrook Academy of Art and was a well-known abstract expressionist painter. She studied with Willem de Kooning at the college and continued to work with him after leaving. She was married to artist Milton Resnick and was active in The Club in New Yotk.

NAME: Louis Selders
BIO: Louis Selders attended Black Mountain for the spring of 1947 as a transfer student from LeMoyne College, and he intended to return to LeMoyne after the spring. He shared in his application to Black Mountain that he was the Student Director of Drama at LeMoyne, “which entitled making scenery, costumes and other things necessary for play production.” He also stated that he was interested in the relationships between minority groups of Jews and Negroes and aimed to learn from attending “the true meaning of the brotherhood of man.”

NAME: Olivia Silberberg
BIO: Olivia Silberberg Toubman (1921-2020) was cellist with the Oklahoma Symphony Orchestra when she was invited to perform at the 1949 summer session at BMC. Silberberg received her soloist diploma from the Longy School of Music in Cambridge, Massachusetts in 1942. The four Silberberg sisters formed the Silberberg String Quartette, which performed in the Boston and Worcester area. She was a member of the Boston Pops Orchestra, the Oklahoma Symphony, and the Atlanta Symphony, as well as chamber groups.

BIO: Concetta Scaravaglione was guest faculty of sculpture at the 1946 Summer Art Institute. Scaravaglione had a long career a sculptor and teacher. While she worked in wood, stone, bronze, and copper, her preferred media was terra-cotta. As her work evolved, it became more abstract.

NAME: Dorothy Cole
BIO: Dorothy was a student from the fall of 1945 to the spring of 1947. While a student, she participated in student concerts and worked in the music library. She took a range of coursework in the arts, music, and social sciences. All of her faculty notes were very positive, across all subjects, and Josef Albers was especially impressed in her work. After her time at BMC, she went on to do illustrations and textile design.

NAME: Roger Sessions
BIO: Roger Sessions was guest faculty of music in the summer of 1944. Sessions was an American composer, teacher, and writer on music. He had initially started his career writing in a neoclassical style, but gradually moved further towards more complex harmonies and postromanticism, and finally the twelve-tone serialism of the Second Viennese School.

NAME: Theodore "Ted" Dreier
BIO: Theodore “Ted” Dreier was the son of a prominent family of idealists committed to the arts, social action, women’s rights, and poverty programs. Ted studied electrical engineering at Harvard University from 1920-1925 then worked at General Electric until 1930. A friend, John Malcolm Forbes, brought Dreier to Rollins College where he taught until 1933. Along with the group of staff that left Rollins with John Andrew Rice, he then joined the founding faculty of Black Mountain College in the summer of 1933.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Physical Sciences/ Math
Role: Faculty, Admin

NAME: Robert Babcock
BIO: Robert Babcock was an educator and politician, that served as a Vermont senate from 1951 to 1955. At Black Mountain College, he taught government and politics from 1940 to 1942. He left in 1942 to work at the United States Department of Treasury as a commercial control specialist. After serving in the Navy during World War II, he returned to teaching at the University of Vermont and obtained his PhD from Northwestern University.

NAME: Mollie Boring
BIO: Mollie Boring was a student at the college for three semester from 1943 through 1944. She took general coursework in all fields and her reports stated that she was unhappy and oftentimes resentful to authority and social interactions on campus. These issues were shared in a letter to her parents and she ultimately left BMC to attend Bard College.

NAME: Massen Noland
BIO: Neil Noland (1927-2013) was the youngest of the three Noland brothers — Harry, Ken and Neil — who enrolled at Black Mountain College (BMC). Noland took a general curriculum with an emphasis on art, music and ceramics. He left to spend a year in Europe on the GI Bill. After a time in Washington D.C., he moved to New York and studied ceramics at Greenwich House while supporting himself working for newspapers and the Arab Information Center. In ceramics,he explored the fish form as an avenue to abstraction. In Amagansett, he worked in various media, including metal and fiberglass.
Relations: Sibling: Kenneth Noland, Sibling: Harry Noland, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Neil in 1997 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

NAME: Nathaniel "Nat" Lytle
BIO: Nat Lytle was a Black Mountain native (raised in Black Mountain and Asheville) who worked for Black Mountain College for over a decade. In the summers of 1933 and 1934, Nat Lytle worked odd jobs and some cooking at the college. From 1937-1938, he worked as an assistant cook, under Jack and Rubye Lipsey, in the kitchen. After three years in the army, he returned to the college from 1944-1946 and worked in the kitchen and on odd jobs. On Tuesdays, the day off for cooks Cornelia and George Williams, Nat Lytle would help remaining cook Malrey Few with the cooking.

NAME: Frank Hursh, Jr.
BIO: Frank Hursh (b. 1929) is an artist, educator, and animator. Hursh enrolled at Black Mountain College in 1949 to study art. He earned a Bachelor of Fine Arts degree at the University of Texas at Austin. Hursh used his G.I. Bill benefits after service in the United States Navy to continue his studies in Mexico. Finding his budding career to be more interesting than his academic studies, he pursued his interests as a freelance commercial artistand animator. At Gamma Studios, he was involved in productions, including Rocky and His Friends (Rocky and Bullwinkle). He taught at the Instituto Allende in San Miguel de Allende, Guanajuato. In Querétaro, he created the arts program at the new campus of the Universidad del Valle de México. Hursh’s paintings and drawings are in an Abstract Expressionist style.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Fall 1949-1950: Color with W.P. Jennerjahn, Design with W.P. Jennerjahn, Theory of Knowledge with Dehn, Painting with Fiore. Spring 1949-1950: Painting with Fiore, Ethics with Dehn, Piano with Hetherington, Colorwith W.P. Jennerjahn

NAME: Renate Benfey
BIO: Renate Benfey Wilkins arrived in the United States with her parents, Eduard and Lotte Benfey, in December 1939. Until Kristallnacht in 1938, her family had not felt threatened by the Nazis. Though Jewish by Hitler’s definition, their faith was Christian. Her father was both a World War I veteran and a judge in Berlin.
Relations: Aunt: Anni Albers, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Renate in 2001 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

NAME: Donald "Don" Wight

Relations: Sister: Earlene Ann Wight
COURSE TAKEN: Fall 1945-1946: Drawing with Albers, Philosophy and the Modern World withLevi, Woodworking with Gregory Winter 1945-1946: Design with Albers, Problems of Philosophy with Dehn, Descriptive Geometry with Dehn, Woodworking with Gregory Summer 1945-1946: Design with Albers, Introduction to Music with M. Fax,Woodworking with Gregory, Geometry with Dehn, Problems of Philosophy withDehn Fall 1946-1947: Weaving II with Mayer, Psychology of Social Issues with Wallen, Introduction to Music with Schlesinger, Chorus with Schlesinger [took leave of absence from school]  Asheville Art Museum Collection Writings about Don can be seen in digitized college bulletins on Asheville Art Museum's collection website: collection.ashevilleart.org. They can be found by searching these accession numbers:  2017.40.039 March 1947 bulletin, " Don Wight and Dan Rice, having trekked to California for golf, found Tommy Cutshaw at the piano."  2017.40.214 March 1946 bulletin, "Dada decorations and costumes, and a Time dance-drama were special business for the Valentine’s Party this year. An elaborate bone-mobile, executed by Vera Baker and Willie Joseph, and an enormous abstract figure in wood, by Don Wight , complicated the ball. The Time dance-drama was very exciting: text by John Reiss, read by Tommie Cutshaw; choreography by Molly Gregory and Patsy Lynch, with the aid of Don Wight and Jose Yglesias; to the beat of Jimmy Tite’s drums."  2017.30.219 November 1946 bulletin, "Willi Joseph, Joan Sihvenen and Don Wight are working on large looms. Willi has designed a tapestry and Don has done experimental weaving using Christmas tinsel and cellophane thread…..Ray Johnson had an exhibition of paintings in his study. The paintings were composed from sketches done in the Jean Varda painting class in the summer Art Institute. After this exhibition, Don Wight planned record concerts and art showings for Sunday evenings in the art room."  2017.40.246 Exhibit of Evolution of Housing Plans of BMC, 1946 "TRANSITION June 1946 Material Exhibited: Drawings of small study-dormitory unit by Don Wight As a result of plans for a small dormitory designed by Don Wight with the collaboration of several other students, the question of the most appropriate size for a new dormitory was re-opened. It was also suggested that studies and sleeping quarters be combined in small units, as shown in Don Wight’s drawings. After much discussion, the latter idea was rejected, but as the consensus of opinion in the community was strongly in favor of a small dormitory to be built first rather than a large one, the campaign to raise funds for the latter was suspended and the whole matter postponed until a new plan could be made."
Focus: Architecture
Role: Student
Start Year: 1945.0
End Year: 1947.0

NAME: Wilma Stokely
BIO: Wilma Dykeman (1920-2006) was a writer of fiction and non-fiction who chronicled the culture of Appalachia in Western North Carolina. Her three novels are The Tall Woman (1962), The Far Family (1966) and Return of the Innocent Earth (1973). Her non-fiction books include The French Broad (1955), and Prophet of Plenty: the First Ninety Years of Willis Weatherford. In the fall of 1944, at Black Mountain College, she and her husband James Dykeman audited classes taught by Alfred Kazin as well as a class taught by poet George Zabriskie, in the fall of 1944.
Relations: Husband: James Stokely, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Wilma in 2002 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

Focus: General Studies

NAME: Sybil Yamins
BIO: Sybil was a student from 1938 to 1940. She participated in Waiting for Lefty in 1940, cast as the secretary. After leaving Black Mountain, she graduated from the School of Business at Simmons College in 1943 and began secretarial work at the Red Cross in New York City. She married Simon Goldberg and devoted her life to raising her children and participating with nonprofit organizations.

NAME: Thomas "Tom" Nee
BIO: Thomas “Tom” Nee (1920- 2008) enrolled at Black Mountain College (BMC) to study with composer Stefan Wolpe. Nee was chair of the department of music at the University of California at San Diego where he developed an innovative contemporary music curriculum. He was artistic director and conductor of the La Jolla Symphony for thirty-one years and director of the New Hampshire Music Festival from 1960 until 1992.
Relations: Wife: Mary Nee, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Thomas and Mary in 1997 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

NAME: Paul Alexander
BIO: Paul Alexander (1931-2021) enrolled at Black Mountain College for its last full academic year, 1955-1956. He had to leave before the end of the year when GI Bill benefits were cut. He settled in San Francisco where he was a central figure in the San Francisco Renaissance. He operated Buzz Gallery and held various jobs, including working for Southern Pacific railroad and in the modern art department at Gump’s. He lived in Fort Bragg in MendocinoCounty in the final years of his life.

NAME: Marcel Dick
BIO: Marcel Dick was a viola and violin player and had a prominent performing career in Europe with string quartets and orchestras before immigrating to the United States. In Europe, he was a member of the Schoenberg Circle and was invited to perform with Rudolf Kolisch’s summer quartet at Black Mountain College. He was head of the graduate department of theory and composition at the Cleveland Institute of Music and principal violinist with the Cleveland Orchestra.

NAME: Sarah Towery
BIO: Sarah Carlisle Towery was a student at Black Mountain College during the 1944 Summer Art Institute. A painter and teacher, she conducted kindergarten and art classes in her home for fifty years. To celebrate her eightieth birthday, in October 1992, her children organized a gathering of professional friends with whom she had studied and painted over many years. That gathering was the catalyst for the Sarah Carlisle Towery/Alabama Art Colony on Lake Martin in Alexander City.

He read the article, applied and was both accepted and granted a tuition reduction.

Mary Emma Harris wrote this short bio for BMC Project, "Lewis worked in computer science at a number of institutions, including the Pentagon, Aberdeen Proving Ground, ENIAC, Douglas Aircraft and the Institutefor Advanced Study in Princeton. After receiving his Ph.D. in Applied Mathematics from the Courant Institute, he worked as Director of Computer Sciences Section at the National Center for Atmospheric Research and as Director of the Computer Sciences Laboratory at the University of Southern California."

BIO: Robert Marden was a student from 1942 to 1943, until he was drafted. He was very active in drama productions while at the college, including Ethan Frome. He also gave the nightly fifteen-minute weekly news summary and commentary to the student body after dinner and was a student officer that regularly sat in on faculty meetings. Bob took his senior examinations in fall of 1942.
Relations: Wife: Helen Grace Wright, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Robert in 1997 and the transcript is available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript

BIO: Barbara Ann Beatty was a student in the fall of 1935 to the spring of 1939. NAME: David Bailey
BIO: David studied at Harvard Unversity before coming to Black Mountain. David studied English and focused on Shakespeare in his studies, at both colleges. He failed his senior examinations the first time and took examinations in Shakespeare at Harvard University in 1934. He submit his writtten senior examinations again in 1935 and graduated. In 1936, he worked with Yale University, then with Miquon, a progressive school for small children in Pennsylvania. He returned to Black Mountain from 1938 to 1940 to work as the Publicity Chairman.

NAME: Viola Farber
BIO: Viola was a dance student from fall 1951 to summer 1953. Viola studied music with Lou Harrison and dance with Katherine Litz. After studying with Merce Cunningham in the summer of 1952, she moved to New York to continue her studies. She returned to BMC the following summer to study with Cunningham and was a founding member of the Merce Cunningham Dance Company. She created many roles for the Cunningham Dance Company and also worked with other choreographers,including Paul Taylor and Katherine Litz. In 1968, she formed The Viola Farber Dance Company.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Fall 1951:Dance (Litz), Music (Harrison), Philosophy (Dehn), Piano (Harrison), Work Program (Billing) Spring 1952: Composition (Harrison), Dance II (Litz), French Tutorial (Shephard), Piano Tutorial (Harrison) Summer 1952: Dance (Litz and Harrison) Summer 1953: Dance Composition (Cunningham), Dance Technique (Cunningham), Dance Repertory (Cunningham), Music for Dancers (Wolpe)

NAME: William "Al" Lanier
BIO: Albert Lanier was born in a small town in Georgia. At the age of sixteen he enrolled at Georgia Tech to study architecture. He interrupted his studies to serve in the United States Navy. After service he returned to Georgia Tech, but decided along with other dissatisfied students including Ragland Watkins and Si Sillman to enroll at Black Mountain to study art and architecture.

NAME: Kenneth Ayres, Jr.
BIO: Kenneth Richard Ayres was a student at the college from the fall of 1939 to the spring of 1941. Kenneth's student file shows that he was very interested in planes and interested in someday becoming a pilot. He studied mathematics and sciences and seemed to have a rough start at his coursework, but improved as he continued his studies at Black Mountain.
Relations: Wife: Phoebe Perry
COURSE TAKEN: Fall Term (1939-1940): Mathematics (Lindsley), General Chemistry (Lindsley), Chorus (Jalowetz), Music I (Evarts), Piano Lessons (Lindsley) Spring (1939-1940): Dramatics (Wunsch), Calculus (Lindsley), Introductory (Lindsley),Beginning German (Straus), Music (Evarts), Chorus (Jalowetz), Grammar (Kurtz), Vocabulary Building (Wunsch) Fall (1940-1941): Intermediate Calculus (Dreier), General Biology (Carpenter), Introduction to Economic Thinking (Gothe), Music (Evarts) Piano Lessons (Evarts),Chorus (Jelowetz) Spring (1940-1941): Math Analysis (Drier), Introduction to Economic Thinking (Gothe), General Biology (Carpenter), Music (Evarts)

NAME: Patricia Nelson
BIO: Patricia Nelson Newhall (1933-2016) was a psychiatrist. In New York after leaving Black Mountain College (BMC), she worked at Motorcycles Incorporated, a shop in New York owned by her future husband Daniel Newhall and a place where other BMC students worked. Newhall studied at City College of New York and Temple University. She earned her medical degree from Women’s Medical College of Philadelphia and graduated from the Philadelphia Psychoanalytic Society. She graduated Summa cum Laude at the Tyler Art School of Art and Architecture where she majored in sculpture.
Relations:

Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft

NAME: Jacqueline Hermann
BIO: Jacqueline was a student in the summer of 1950, while still a student at the High School of Music and Art in New York. She studied at the Art Students League and at the Art Institute of Chicago. As a painter, her interest has been in observation and interpretation of landscapes and cityscapes. She groups her paintings in three categories: city paintings, cloud paintings and terrain/in flight paintings.She is a member of the National Academy of Arts and Letters in New York and, in 2004, she was recipient of the Arts and Letters Award.

NAME: Jose Yglesias
BIO: Jose was a student in 1946. While a student, he served as a Student Officer as Secretary, on the Discipline Committee, and also participated in a student-run French club. He participated in the Community Council in "preparing a program of community education which will lead to a democratic decision by the community as to how the emergency buildings given to us by the Federal Government are to be utilized," in which he was a secretary.

NAME: Raymond "Ray" Toubman
BIO: Raymond Toubman (1921-2009) was first oboist with the Oklahoma Symphony Orchestra when he was invited to perform at the 1949 summer session at Black Mountain College (BMC). During his career, he was first oboist with the Indianapolis Symphony, the Southern Symphony Orchestra, and the Atlanta Symphony. He performed with the Boston Pops Orchestra under Arthur Fielder and with many Boston organizations. Toubman obtained his master’s degree in library science and his Ph.D. in musicology from Boston University. While working as librarian at the Oglethorpe University Library, Toubman performed as a freelance musician.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Music

NAME: Elizabeth "Lisa" Jalowetz
BIO: Lisa Jalowetz Aronson (1920-2013) was a stage designer and collaborator with Boris Aronson for major Broadway productions including A Little Night Music, Fiddler on the Roof, The Crucible and many others. She worked with notable theater persons including Harry Horner and Norman Bel Geddes. She collaborated with Frank Rich on The Theatre of Boris Aronson, documenting his work as a stage designer. As daughter of conductor Heinrich Jalowetz, she spent her childhood in Austria and Germany. When the Nazis invaded Vienna where she was a student, she escaped to Holland. A student visa from Black Mountain College where her father had been appointed enabled her to immigrate to the United States.
Relations: Parent: Johanna Jalowetz, Parent: Heinrich Jalowetz, Sister: Trude Guermonprez, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Lisa in 2000 (transcript) and 2006 (transcript) which are available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Performance Art
Role: Student
Start Year: 1939.0
End Year: 1940.0

NAME: William Miller
BIO: William Blackall Miller (1916-2004) graduated in art history from Harvard University and received his Ph.D. from Columbia University. He taught at the Rhode Island School of Design, Milton Academy, Amherst College, and Colby College. Miller, besides being an artist, was an accomplished calligrapher and a pop craftsman creating limericks, whirligigs, hand bound books, toys and puzzles. He spent a year in Rome on a Fulbright Scholarship and traveled widely. He was active in the Maine League of Historical Societies and Museums and other preservation organizations.

NAME: John Rice
BIO: John Andrew Rice was an educator and founder of Black Mountain College. He was born and raised in South Carolina; his father was a Methodist minister and college educator, and his mother was sister to U.S. Senator, Ellison Durant “Cotton Ed” Smith. Rice attended the Webb School in Tennessee, a college preparatory boarding school, and Tulane University in New Orleans, where he graduated with a bachelors in classics in just three years. He was awarded a Rhodes Scholarship in 1911, attended Oxford University until 1914, and was a doctoral student in classics from the University of Chicago from 1916 to 1917.
Relations: Wife: Nell Rice, Child: Frank Rice, Child: Mary Rice

NAME: Claude Stoller
BIO: Claude Stoller was an architect of note, known for his advocacy for social housing and historic preservation. He grew up in the Bronx, New York. Stoller's completion of studies at DeWitt Clinton High School, coincided with the exhibit Bauhaus 1919-1928 at the Museum of Modern Art. Through this exhibition, he became interested in attending Black Mountain College (BMC) in western North Carolina. While at BMC, Stoller took a wide variety of classes, including Josef Albers's courses in design, color, and drawing along with architecture courses led by Lawrence Kocher, Howard Dearstyne, and Lou Bernard Voight. Stoller, along with fellow student Charles Forberg, oversaw the construction of the Jalowetz House, a small dwelling designed by Lawrence Kocher for music instructor Henrich Jalowetz, and his family. BMC also gave Stoller a chance to experiment with photography. In 1942, Claude Stoller was drafted into the United States Army. He served in the 14th Coast Artillery on Puget Sound in Washington State. After attending army engineering school, Stoller served in Germany and France with the 13th Armored Division.
Relations: Married BMC students Nan Oldenburg, then Rosemary Raymond.: Nan Oldenburg, Mary Emma Harris interviewed Claude in 1998 (transcript), 2002 (transcript), an 2012 (transcript) which are available from Appalachian State University under The Mary Emma Harris and Black Mountain College Project, Inc. Oral History collection.: transcript
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Architecture
Role: Student
Start Year: 1939.0
End Year: 1943.0

BIO: Franziska “Frances” de Graaff was born October 11, 1904 in Leyden, Holland. She studied Russian, romance languages and literature and received her doctorate in language and literature from the University of Leyden in 1933. From 1933 until 1936 she worked in Moscow as a translator and continued her studies on Russian poet Sergey Esenin. She returned to Holland and taught Russian and French.

NAME: Adele Suska
BIO: Adele Suska LaBreque was a student at Black Mountain College for the 1950-51 academic year and for the 1951 summer session. At the end of the summer she left for San Francisco with friends. She then returned to Black Mountain in the summer of 1952.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Fall 1950-1951:Design with Jennerjahn, Color with Jennerjahn, Drawing with Fiore, Dance with B. Jennerjahn, Weaving with Oates Spring 1950-1951: Dance Technique with B. Jennerjahn, Color with Jennerjahn, Textile Design, Weaving with Oates Summer 1951: Dance with Litz  Obituary: Adele LaBrecque during her nearly 94 years studied art at Black Mountain College in North Carolina, danced the flamenco in New York City, and journeyed to Dharan, Saudi Arabia, as a single woman in the 1950s to work in public relations for Aramco Oil Co. She was just getting started. From there, she married Maurice LaBrecque, who she met in Dharan, had two sons – Louis and Mark – and lived in Tripoli, Libya, from 1966 to 1984 during a time of immense change in the country. The family traveled widely, and Adele immersed herself in her art, whether it was painting scenes of everyday life in Saudi Arabia or filming and acting as editor and producer of homemade documentary films in both Saudi Arabia and Libya. Adele and Maury moved to Winston-Salem, N.C., after leaving Libya following Maury’s retirement, and Adele ultimately worked in Wake Forest University’s alumni office until her own retirement in early 1999, more than nine years after her husband’s death in December 1989. At that point, she headed to Santa Fe, a place where she felt completely at home. Volunteer work was a given for Adele, and she put her talents to use at a variety of organizations, including the Santa Fe Opera and the Lensic Performing Arts Center. Adele continued to paint and found a wonderful group of friends who enriched her life.
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Student
Start Year: 1950.0
End Year: 1951.0

NAME: Martin "Rudy" Haase

NAME: Ruth Miller
BIO: Ruth Miller was a student from 1942 to the 1944 Summer Music Institute. Mary Emma Harris wrote a short bio of Ruth with the BMC Project interviews, sharing that she "studied dance with Elsa Kahl at Black Mountain College. In New York, she studied with Doris Humphrey and José Limón and, in 1949, she became a principal dancer with the Limón Company. From 1972 to 1978, she was director of the Company. In 1958, Currier formed her own dance company, while continuing to dance with Limón. Miller Currier also taught at Juilliard’s Dance Division."

Start Year: 1942.0
End Year: 1944.0

NAME: Alma Stone Williams
BIO: Alma Stone Williams was a musician, educator, music scholar, and pioneer in racial integration. Williams attended the 1944 Summer Music Institute. She was the first African-American student to be enrolled at Black Mountain College. Before attending BMC, she was a student at Spelmen College and Atlanta University. Her tuition was covered through the Rosenwald Fund, which also paid her way through Juliard. She was already a music teacher at Fort Valley State College when she applied to BMC. She also obtained a masters in musicology at the University of Maryland where she focused on the works of Brahms.

Focus: Music

NAME: Clemens Kalischer
BIO: Clemens Kalischer was born in Lindau, Germany, in 1921. In 1933, he and his family fled Nazi Germany and relocated to Paris. After the Nazi occupation of Paris, he was held in various French concentration camps. In 1942, after reconnecting with his father at a concentration camp, they escaped and made their way to Casablanca. In Morocco, they found passage to the United States.

NAME: Sophie Hunt French
BIO: Sophie Hunt French (1915-2009) came to Black Mountain College with her husband John R.P. French, a former student who had returned to teach psychology. After her son lost his hearing, she obtained her master’s degree from Columbia University in special education. In Ann Arbor, Michigan, she was hired by Eastern Michigan University to work with deaf children and their families and trained students to work with deaf children.

NAME: Charles "Chuck" Forberg

NAME: Richard "Dick" Bush-Brown

NAME: Gisela Kronenberg
BIO: At Black Mountain College, Gisela Kronenberg Herwitz took a general curriculum. She graduated in 1943 in psychology. In Chicago and Cincinnati, she worked as a research assistant in medical schools and hospitals while taking graduate courses. She later moved to Peekshill. There she reared her two daughters and was active in community activities. She was president of the PTA and on the Boards of the United Way and the Girl Scout Council. She later was Executive Director of Peekskill YWCA and worked for the Westchester County Department of Health in the office of Quality Assurance.
Relations:

NAME: Leo Amino
BIO: Leo Amino—born in Taiwan in 1911 where his father was an agricultural consultant for the Japanese government—was reared in Tokyo. In 1929, he immigrated to the United States and studied at San Mateo Junior College in California for two years and, later, at New York University. He remained in New York to work for a Japanese wood importing firm and took home ebony samples to carve. Although he had received no formal art training, his interest in sculpture grew rapidly and, in 1937, he studied briefly at the American Artists School with Chaim Gross, a leading proponent of direct carving.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Guest Faculty
Start Year: 1946.0
End Year: 1950.0

NAME: Caroline Ewing
BIO: Caroline Burton Michahelles (b. 1929) was a real estate agent, an artist, and a poet. She attended the University of Pennsylvania where she majored in philosophy and the Fashion Institute of Technology in New York. After her marriage to Nicolo Michahelles, she lived in Connecticut and Italy. In 1973, she organized an influential exhibition of American artists in Europe at the Palazzo Pretorio in Prato, Tuscany, Italy. After her children were grown, she focused on her painting. Her poetry has been published as Nonsense-no Poems.

NAME: Harriet Florence Sohmers
BIO: Harriet Sohmers Zwerling (1928-2019) was a writer, editor, and artist’s model. In Paris in the 1950s, she was associated with the expatriate scene and later part of the literary scene in New York. She was an artist’s model for painters. While a student at Berkeley, she was a friend of Peggy Tolk-Watkins, owner of the Tin Angel, a jazz and crab bar in Sausalito. She obtained her B.A. from Hunter College and, after her marriage to Louis Zwerling,taught elementary school in Brooklyn, New York, for twenty-eight years. She was bisexual and had affairs with Susan Sontag, Irene Fornés and others. Her memoir Notes of a Nude Model and Other Pieces was published in 2003. She appears in the documentary Still Doing It about the sex lives of older women.
Relations:

NAME: Etta Mandelbaum
BIO: Etta Deikman notably studied painting with Hans Hoffman at his School of Art in New York City. While at Black Mountain College in the 1948 summer session, she studied painting with Willem de Kooning and sculpture with Peter Grippe. She was there for the legendary summer of John Cage, Merce Cunningham, and Buckminster Fuller who were also brought on by Josef Albers to teach. Deikman approaches each painting as an adventurous journey, finding surprising relationships and ideas as she goes; this sort of freedom of thought and acceptance of chance aligns with the way students were taught at Black Mountain College.

BIO: Soon after Morton Steinau graduated from Louisville Male High School in Kentucky in January 1935, he and Bela Martin, another graduate, hitchhiked in bitter April weather from Louisville to Black Mountain College where Robert Wunsch was teaching English. They remained for two months.

NAME: Thomas Ayres, Jr.
BIO: Thomas Owen Ayres, Jr. was a student at the college from the fall of 1949 to the spring of 1950. Thomas left during his first semester and was admitted to The Institute for Living in Hartford, CT, for "emotional disturbance characterized by anxiety, disinterest, and withdrawal from social contacts" which also led to his withdraw from the Navy two years prior. After leaving the Institute, he came back to Black Mountain in the spring of 1951, but also left during that semester and did not return.

NAME: Edward "Ed" Dorn
BIO: Edward Dorn was a poet and teacher. Studied at the University of Illinois and at Black Mountain College (1950-1955). At Black Mountain he came into contact with Charles Olson, who greatly influenced his literary worldview and his sense of himself as poet. Dorn's final examiner at Black Mountain was Robert Creeley (q.v.), with whom, along with the poet Robert Duncan, Dorn became included as one of a trio of younger poets later associated with Black Mountain and with Charles Olson. Joined the teaching staff at the University of Essex in 1965. He spent most of the next five years in England, where he published several collections of poems and wrote Book 1 of of his most famous poem 'Gunslinger'
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: English/ Writing
Role: Student
Start Year: 1950.0

NAME: Isaac Cramer
BIO: Isaac Doughton Cramer was born March 27, 1915 in Camden, New Jersey. He attended the Moorestown Friends’ School in New Jersey from 1929 to 1933. On the recommendation of Frank Aydelotte, President of Swarthmore College and brother-in-law of John Andrew Rice, he enrolled at 40 Black Mountain College in September 1933, making him one of the first students at the new college. He graduated on December 7, 1937.

NAME: Kenneth Snelson
BIO: Ken Snelson grew up in Pendleton, Oregon where his father John Tavner Snelson had a camera shop. After his discharge from the navy, he enrolled at the University of Oregon in Eugene to study painting. He remembers Jack Wilkinson, the head of the art department, as a man with far-ranging interests and an interesting mind. It was through Wilkinson that he was first introduced to Bauhaus ideas.

NAME: Annelise "Anni" Albers
BIO: Annelise Elsa Freida Fleischmann was born in Berlin, Germany in 1899 and grew up in a comfortable well-to-do family. Her father worked as a furniture maker. Intrigued with design and aesthetics at an early age, she became interested in the idea of becoming an artist. In 1922, she enrolled at the Bauhaus in Weimar. Although originally interested in painting, she was steered towards textiles, as were most women.

NAME: Hazel Larsen
BIO: Hazel Larsen Archer experienced life at BMC as a student, faculty, and as a staff member working in the college office. She studied art, design, and photography, taking classes with Josef Albers, Josef Breitenbach, and Beaumont Newhall among others. Starting in 1949, Hazel was appointed to teach photography, and was at the college for nine years altogether. As many of her students later stated, Hazel had a lasting impact on them with her emphasis on seeing rather than technique. She also invited photographers to teach at Black Mountain College including Harry Callahan, Aaron Siskind, and Arthur Siegel for a special focus on photography in the summer of 1951.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Summer 1944: Composition with Jean Charlot, Drawing and Painting with Charlot, Color with J. Albers, General Design with J. Albers, Photography with J.Breitenbach Summer1945:Writing with Margret Beidler Fall 1945-1946: Drawing with Albers, Painting with Albers Winter 1945-1946: Painting with Albers, Design with Albers, Shorthand with Alice Rondthaler Spring 1945-1946: Painting with Albers, Design with Albers, Shorthand with A. Rondthaler, Work Program Summer 1946: Color with Albers Spring 1947-1948: Design with Albers Summer 1948: Photography with Newhall, Architecture and IndustrialEngineering with Fuller
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Faculty, Student, Staff

NAME: Harrison Begay
BIO: Harrison Begay was a student for the 1940-1941 school year under a scholarship from the Indian Commission. Harrison spoke with the Director of Education at the US Department of the Interior, Office of Indian Affairs, and shared that he wanted to "master the White man's culture, while remaining Indian." Though, he shared that he was not interested in furthering training in his arts, but he wanted to learn more English and about a culture outside of his own. Begay also taught a Navajo class while a student.
Relations:

NAME: Harriet Engelhardt
BIO: Harriet Engelhardt grew up in a prominent Montgomery, Ala.,family. She attended the Margaret Booth School in Montgomery and later went to Hollins College and Black Mountain College. During World War II she became a Red Cross worker in Europe. She was the driver of a clubmobile which closely followed U. S. troops across France, Germany, and Austria in 1944 and 1945. She was killed in October 1945 in a jeep accident the day before she was scheduled to return to the United States.

NAME: Ernst Krenek
BIO: Ernst Heinrich Krenek was an Austrian, later American, composer. He explored atonality and other modern styles and wrote a number of books, including Music Here and Now, a study of Johannes Ockeghem, and Horizons Circled: Reflections on my Music. Krenek wrote two pieces using the pseudonym Thornton Winsloe.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: 
Focus: Music
Role: Guest Faculty
Start Year: 1944.0
End Year: 1944.0

NAME: Donald "Don" Alter
BIO: Donald Alter shared in his application, “by attending Black Mountain College I hope to help myself become more mundane. I hope to become more independent in my thinking and living. I also believe it is important for an individual to learn of his relation with cooperative community life. In the final analysis I believe that all the things I hope to gain at Black Mountain would help me to become a more aware and more worthwhile artist. From the information I have gathered about Black Mountain I have concluded that the program offered is conductive to the afore said.”
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Fall Term 1948-1949: Drawing (Albers), Painting (Albers), Elementary Music (Bodky), Elementary German (Rice) Spring Term 1948-1949: Color (Albers), Russian tutorial (Goldowski), Bookbinding (Jalowetz), Writing (Olson), Textile Construction I (Guermonprez & Albers), German(Bodky) Fall Term 1949-1950: Book Binding (Jalowetz), Painting (Fiore), Drawing (Fiore), Design (Jennerjahn), Geometry for Artists (Dehn) Spring term 1949-1950: Painting (Fiore), Design (Jennerjahn), Drawing (Fiore) Summer Term 1950: Color (Jennerjahn), Photography (Oates & Larson) Fall Term 1950: Philosophy (Dehn), Advanced Color and Design Tutorial (Jennerjahn), Painting (Fiore), Drawing (Fiore), Reading (Richards) Spring Term 1951: Drawing (Fiore), Painting tutorial (Fiore), Seminar Methods, Trends in Contemporary Painting (Fiore), Light Sound Movement (Pete and Betty Jennerjahn)
Focus: Art/ Design/ Craft
Role: Student
Start Year: 1948.0
End Year: 1951.0

NAME: John McGraw, Jr.
BIO: John McGraw was a student from fall of 1933 to spring of 1938.
Relations: 
COURSE TAKEN: Fall 1933-1934: Ancient History with Boyden, Appreciation ofMusic with Evarts, Chaucer with Martin, French with Keith. Winter 1933-1934: French Conversation with Keith, Greek andRoman History with Boyden, Drawing with Albers, and Music Appreciation with Evarts. Spring 1933-1934: Music Appreciation with Evarts, French conversation with Keith, Drawing with Albers and Greek History and Humanism with Boyden. Fall 1935-1936 [note year gap]: Survey of French Literature with Mangold, Creative Writing with Wunsch, Drawing II with Albers, MusicAppreciation with Evarts, Plato II with Rice, and teaching shop 2 days a week. Spring 1935-1936: Survey of French Literature with Mangold, Play Writing with Wunsch, Plato II with Rice, Music II with Evarts, Shakespeare and Elizabethan Drama with Martin and Wunsch, Teaching at cottage school two days per week. Fall 1936-1937: U.S. History with King, 17th Century English literature with Martin, Dramatics II with Wunsch, England since 1485 with King. Spring 1936-1937: American History with King, Contemporary American Literature with Wunsch, Dramatics II with Wunsch, Survey of English Literature with Martin. Fall 1937-1938: Writing Seminar with Rice, Dramatics II and III with Wunsch, American Literature Tutorial with Wunsch. Spring 1937-1938: Dramatics II with Wunsch, Seminar inAmerican Literature with Surette, Writing Seminar with Rice, Tutorial inAmerican Literature with Wunsch.

NAME: Erwin Straus
BIO: Erwin Straus was a German-American phenomenologist and neurologist who helped to pioneer anthropological medicine and psychiatry, a holistic approach to medicine that is critical of mechanistic and reductionistic approaches to understanding and treating human beings.
